note: The exact first-error timestamp is not pinned. The funnel is bucketed at 10 min and shows the first broker failures in the 09:20-09:30 buckets. The conversation_trace query returned 8 of 17 records (truncated), and the earliest returned is 09:42:41, so earlier failures were not inspected.
note: No time-series points were pulled for worker latency_ms or err_rate (summary only), and wa-webhook metrics and logs were not queried. There is therefore no latency chart and no wa-webhook view. Worker latency of 2400-3317ms is only a window summary; any step change at 09:40 cannot be confirmed.
note: Data begins at 09:00 (zero traffic 08:30-09:00), so a pre-09:00 baseline is unavailable.
note: The broker, aa-api, redis-session and llm-gateway services were not examined, so the cause of the broker timeouts is unverified.
note: Only 8 traces were sampled, all order-status. The 40% order-status failure rate means some order-status requests succeed, and I did not determine what distinguishes failing from succeeding ones (user, symbol or time). All 8 sampled users differ, which argues against a user subset but is not conclusive.
note: I exceeded the suggested budget of about 5 calls, with 7 calls made.
note: broker-api had no WARN+ logs, which is not proof of health; INFO/DEBUG logs and broker-api metrics (latency, errors) were not queried, so the slowness cannot be confirmed from the broker side.
note: wa-webhook logs, redis-session metrics, and sqs-outbound metrics were not queried (call budget), so redis-session and wa-webhook symptoms and the component ordering are unverified.
note: sqs-inbound and rds-postgres were checked only as window summaries; I did not pull time-series points, so I cannot show the exact onset time in those metrics.
note: Whether the timeout is a recent config change (0.5s) or a deploy was not checked; that is for the dependency teammate.
note: get_changes returned no config, flag, or migration records in the window, but I cannot rule out sources not covered by that tool (secret or credential rotations, certificate/token/quota expiries, infra or scaling changes, vendor announcements from broker or providers).
note: I did not query dependencies for rds-postgres, redis-session, llm-gateway, aa-api or the queues, so I have not checked for other links to order status.
note: The deploy was recorded at 09:38 UTC while the commit merged at 09:32 UTC; I did not verify when the config actually took effect on running workers.
note: No latency data was checked here to confirm broker-api responses exceed 0.5s.
note: I made more than the budgeted number of calls and did not fetch dependencies for wa-webhook, sender, llm-gateway, redis-session, rds-postgres or the queues. Their upstream/downstream edges are therefore inferred from the worker's edges only.
note: The graph does not say which intent uses which tool, so the order-status vs balance mapping is an assumption. Observability should confirm that failing requests hit the broker tool and that balance requests use aa-api or rds-postgres.
note: No retry policy is visible in the data; only the timeout values in config/app.json are known.
note: The commit merged_at (09:32Z) differs from the deploy ts (09:38Z). I treated 09:38Z as the deploy time. I did not verify broker-api latency, so I cannot confirm that responses exceed 0.5s.
note: The worker, wa-webhook and sender deploy refs were not independently verified as running in production beyond the get_changes record.
note: No past incident involved broker-api, tool-call failures or a one-intent partial outage, so there is no diagnostic precedent.
note: Neither returned incident records a cause that was later ruled out, so I cannot report any ruled-out causes.
note: I did not check current changes (deploy, config, migration, flag) on worker or broker-api near 09:40 UTC, so the trigger is unidentified.
note: I did not run a rds-postgres-specific search. The third query combined several components and returned only two loosely related items.
note: The search results had no source or url fields, so the postmortems cannot be read in full.
note: No p50/p95/p99 breakdown is available. The metric list has only one latency_ms series, a 5-minute resolution was used, and I could not compute the share of requests above 0.5s.
note: The summary reports 1 missing latency point, but all 18 returned points at 5-minute steps have values. I could not locate the gap.
note: I do not know whether latency_ms includes calls the worker abandoned at 0.5s. If it does not, timed-out calls would be under-counted in the latency series.
note: I did not use the worker's per-call 'ms' field from traces for successful calls in 09:00-09:38, which would give a direct distribution against 0.5s. That check is still open.
note: Call volume is very low (about 0.06 rps), so individual 5-minute buckets are noisy.
note: No instance ID or per-instance version label exists in worker logs or metrics, so I could not tie timeouts to specific instances or refs.
note: I could not show whether any instances still ran the old ref (5s timeout) after 09:38:40, or whether their successful broker calls took longer than 0.5s. No successful broker call lines or latency were visible.
note: The timeout search covered only 09:30-09:50, so I did not check for 5s timeouts or a mixed config later, including around the 10:20 bucket at 50%.
note: The version search for 6e7b46e returned one line, so I saw no later restarts or config reloads. I did not search for other restart terms such as 'restart', 'shutdown' or 'config'.
note: I did not query broker-api latency metrics, so the 0.5s timeout versus real broker latency question stays open, and rollout effect versus broker degradation is undecided.
note: Only 20 of 123 order_status traces were seen (truncated), covering about 09:33-09:37. Calls from 09:00-09:33 were not examined, so the distribution over the whole pre-09:38 period is unverified. The sample is also not a random draw.
note: The 35% figure is a small-sample estimate: 7 of 20 gives a wide uncertainty band, roughly 15-60%. A few calls near 500 ms (477 and 510) make the proportion sensitive to the exact threshold.
note: I did not check whether the sampled calls are representative of the whole window or whether latency was drifting upward during 09:33-09:37.
note: The tool offers no per-call latency histogram for the broker tool, so a full distribution would need further paged conversation_trace queries with narrower time windows.
note: Only 5-minute averages were available; no latency percentiles (p95/p99), so the tail above 0.5s cannot be confirmed or ruled out. The 913.5 ms max is not located in time.
note: One latency point is missing (missing:1) and the series has 18 points for a 90-minute window, so a short spike or gap could be hidden. The missing point's timestamp is not identifiable; the 10:30 endpoint is not included.
note: The 09:35 err_rate rise (0.133) precedes the 09:38 deploy time; it may be bucket noise at ~0.06 rps or an earlier precursor (cf. 503s at 09:23 in E-017). The ordering is not resolved.
note: err_rate semantics are unknown (what counts as an error, and whether it is computed server-side), and broker-api logs had no WARN or above, so the cause of the metric's errors is not visible.

Report: ANSWER

The most probable root cause is worker deploy 6e7b46e at 09:38 UTC (PR 334). It cut the broker tool timeout in config/app.json from 5s to 0.5s and left the portfolio and aa timeouts unchanged. broker-api routinely answers in about 400-850 ms, so many order-status broker calls now hit ReadTimeout at exactly 0.5s, and the worker sends the 'Sorry, I couldn't fetch that right now' fallback. broker-api latency shows no step at onset, so a broker-side degradation (H2) fits the evidence poorly. Sporadic broker 503s before the deploy look like a separate low-level issue, not the trigger. The problem is ongoing: timeouts continue to 10:29 and up to half of order-status requests fail. Worker err_rate does not show it because the fallback counts as a successful reply.

rankhypothesiscauseconfidence
1Worker deploy 6e7b46e at 09:38 cut the broker tool timeout from 5s to 0.5s, below broker-api's normal response time, so many order-status broker calls hit ReadTimeout and the worker sends the 'couldn't fetch' fallback; balance is unaffected because the aa and portfolio timeouts were left unchanged.worker/config_timeout_too_tight0.747
2broker-api itself degraded around 09:40, with latency rising above 0.5s and earlier sporadic 503s as a precursor, so the worker's broker calls time out and return the fallback regardless of the worker deploy.broker-api/dependency_latency_degradation0.424
